# Ember #1945 research journal and experiment log

A live record of what the #1945 throughput-and-qualification campaign is trying, what each attempt
showed, and what it did not close. Its purpose is to make the next decision better than the last
one. Read it before proposing any arm, and write to it every time an experiment is ruled.

- **Campaign ledger** (the full history to 2026-09-27, by gate, with repeat detection):
  [campaign.html](campaign.html), source `campaign.md`.
- **Experiment records** (every loop hypothesis, frozen criterion and verdict): [hypotheses.html](hypotheses.html).
- The log below is appended automatically when a loop hypothesis is ruled or updated. Hand entries
  record decisions, corrections and anything a loop stage does not capture.

## How to use this before deciding

1. Search the log and the ledger for the proposition you are about to test, **in its own words**.
   An empty search is not clearance.
2. Check the **Standing lessons**. Each one names a mistake this campaign made more than once.
3. State which gate the experiment moves, its ceiling, and its frozen refutation criterion. Then run it.
4. After the ruling, check that the entry landed here. Add what the result does **not** close.

## Current state

| Gate | Requirement | State | Newest | Best on the current image-inclusive arm |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | complete-update p10 > 80,000 loss-bearing positions/s over ≥1,024 warmed updates | UNMET | 58,499 (attempt 14) | 85,551.3 (attempt 7, earlier mixture) |
| B | aggregate loss-bearing targets / governed wall > 80,000 over a continuous governed hour (not a p10) | UNMET | 61,740 (attempt 14) | 86,662.5 (attempt 7) |
| C | No-worse-learning licence on the measured arm | MET (v3 arm) | −0.505 / −0.472 nats vs control | — |
| D | Protected MMMU > 0.2857 on the hour's trained child | UNMET, never cleared | 0.263 (attempt 14) | 0.285 (attempt 13) |
| E | Frozen admitted production-mixture identity | MET | — | — |
| F | Single RTX 4090, truthful accounting, receipts intact | MET | — | — |

14 numbered attempts, none ACCEPTED. No governed hour may run until both of these exist:
- a measured A/B cure: a matched pair, with a null pair first, showing p90 ≤ 40.6 ms;
- a measured D cure: real MMMU beats both missing-image and wrong-image by more than one item standard error.

## Standing lessons

1. **An instrument is proven before its verdict is trusted.** From attempt 8 to 2026-09-27, every
   missing-image and wrong-image MMMU control ran an evaluator that ignored the image condition. The
   conclusion "the image rarely moves MMMU" was never measured. A control run must record the
   condition it actually executed.
2. **A claim is made by the acceptance consumer**, never by the statistic the arm was built to move.
   Several clearances of 80,000 were read from a row timer that covered only part of each update.
3. **Fixing one gate has repeatedly broken another.** Attempts 7 to 9 met A and B but failed D.
   The v3 mixture that brought D closest (attempts 12 to 14) lost A and B. Judge every attempt on all
   six gates.
4. **Isolated probes overpredict governed gains** (by 5× or more on this campaign). Size a treatment
   with a matched captured pair, null pair first.
5. **No attempt is dispatched while known to fail a gate.** Attempts 11 to 14 were, four times, on
   an unchanged arm.

## Log

Newest first. Automatic entries carry the loop hypothesis id.

2. H19 child, same arms (issue2119-h19-episode-nll-20261004): fresh expected 4.92 (3.9 to 5.8), measured 4.961, HIT. older_early_shards expected 3.26 (2.6 to 4.2), measured 4.442, MISS by 1.18 on the high side: forgetting was +1.879 against a predicted +0.70, 2.7 times the size. Lesson: size a forgetting prediction from the arm of text the model had memorised, not from the aggregate held-out change; keep the in-hour training scalar and a fixed-model score as separate quantities until the same examples are scored. ### 2026-10-02 12:28 PM LA — Correction (hand entry, from the counterpart seat's arithmetic review): the tenth-hour efficiency denominator is physical positions

The two tenth-hour entries below quote learning efficiency "per 100M applied targets" over a denominator of 101,974,016. That denominator is APPLIED PHYSICAL POSITIONS (every decoder position the hour applied), not aggregate loss-bearing targets. The aggregate loss-target efficiency for the tenth hour is therefore UNMEASURED; the quoted value is nats per held-out target per 100M applied physical positions. The caption-set binding for that hour is manifest and counts only (no ordered caption-target digest). The scored values, the frozen bands and the SUPPORTED disposition are unchanged; SUPPORTED there means the 512-pack scoring was valid and inside the bands, not that held-out learning improved. The arithmetic review that found this (the counterpart seat, 10:11 AM LA) came after the 09:46 AM LA ruling, so the agreement between them is retrospective.

## Frozen criterion (unchanged from 41515; pasted verbatim into loop predict before claim)
- caption wrong-real (NLL wrong-image minus NLL real-image): REFUTED if the treatment does not exceed the control
  by 0.05 nats.
- MMMU real-missing and real-wrong, n=847, no score bar. The statistic is the difference-in-differences:
  (treatment real-minus-contrast) minus (control real-minus-contrast).
  - Its interval is computed from PAIRED per-question outcomes, matched across every condition and both arms. It
    comes from a paired item bootstrap, resampling questions and never conditions independently. All per-question
    correctness vectors are saved in the receipt.
  - Exact method (the counterpart seat 42180): the interval is a 95% percentile interval over 10,000 paired bootstrap resamples of
    the 847 questions, seed 0. It is applied to control/treatment, and separately to the control/control null
    pair. The null pair is the INSTRUMENT CHECK, not an additive term. If its interval excludes 0, the run is
    REFUSED as non-discriminating before any verdict, and that check is applied before the treatment pair is read.
  - 0.0154, the one-score standard error, is NOT that interval. It is kept only as a preset OPERATING THRESHOLD:
    the treatment's statistic must exceed +0.0154 to count as a rise. (the counterpart seat 42166 correction, adopted before any
    predict.)
  - DECISION ORDER (amended 2026-10-01 02:06 AM LA on the counterpart seat 42760; mutually exclusive, first match wins; s = the
    statistic, [L,U] = its 95% interval):
    1. Null-pair interval excludes 0 -> REFUSED (instrument does not discriminate; no verdict read).
    2. U < 0 -> REFUTED-HARM (treatment worse than control).
    3. s > +0.0154 and L > 0 -> SUPPORTED (a rise past the operating threshold).
    4. s > +0.0154 and L <= 0 -> INCONCLUSIVE (point past the threshold, interval includes 0).
    5. U < +0.0154 -> REFUTED (failed the preset bar; NOT evidence of harm).
    6. Otherwise -> INCONCLUSIVE.
    Counterexamples: s=-0.002, [-0.010,0.005] -> rule 5 REFUTED. s=0.020, [-0.004,0.044] -> rule 4 INCONCLUSIVE.
    s=0.010, [-0.010,0.030] -> rule 6 INCONCLUSIVE. s=0.030, [0.006,0.054] -> rule 3 SUPPORTED.

### 2026-09-29 07:00 AM LA — SUPPORTED: `optimizer-shadow-bf16-moment-and-update-loss-20260929`
- **Hypothesis:** Mechanism (the counterpart seat 39713/39716): where g^2 is small against v, v_new = 0.999 v + 0.001 g^2 rounds back to the stored BF16 v, so v never decays and only inflates; the step m/sqrt(v) is then too small, and updates below half a ulp of BF16 p are lost. Both erase learning the weights would otherwise show. A one-step FP32 recompute cannot exclude it (both sides inherit the stale v), so the shadow is SEQUENTIAL from the ON child's restored state over the same actual gradients.
- **Frozen criterion:** REFUTED if REFUTED if, at every update N>=128, BOTH stay within 1% on the median block: |v ratio - 1| < 0.01 and the displacement gap < 0.01. Anything between is INCONCLUSIVE. Validity precondition (cannot make any verdict SUPPORTED or REFUTED, only voids it as INSTRUMENT-INVALID): the same-state fp32 emulation must reproduce the fused kernel's stored BF16 p and v bit-for-bit on >= 99% of elements on the median block, and the restored optimizer step counts must equal the manifest's. Numeric prediction (the counterpart seat arithmetic): v ratio 0.77-0.95 at N=128-256, point 0.85; displacement gap 0.05-0.25; P(SUPPORTED) about 0.5. Scope: arithmetic diagnosis only; says nothing about accuracy under a corrected optimizer. Reported separately, no threshold: (a) fraction of v entries whose decay rounds away, (b) fraction of update entries below half a BF16 ulp of p, (c) gradient scale (this path has no clip; accumulation mean only), zero-change fraction, effective update fraction actual vs shadow. - **Ruling:** Frozen criterion met at N=257 (median block): v ratio fp32/bf16 = 0.780 <= 0.95 and displacement gap = 0.561 >= 0.10; instrument valid (bit-exact emulation p 0.9999996, v 1.0; first-update clocks match restored state). ### 2026-09-28 06:23 PM LA — Scope limit: crossed ID probes do not prove current-child training membership
**Scope limit on the 06:17 PM correction (counterpart seat 39036, accepted).**

The two crossed ID receipts load the current child, but they sample 2,000 fixed row IDs from an older A1 manifest. ### 2026-09-28 06:17 PM LA — Record correction: real-vs-blank does not show content use; identity signal acquisition remains open
**Correction (counterpart-seat critique 39022, accepted).** Three earlier entries read the in-distribution result "real 0.5335 vs blank 0.296" as proof that the model uses image CONTENT, and so placed gate D's failure in transfer to college-level items. That inference does not follow from a blank-only comparator. A blank image removes image PRESENCE as well as content, so the gap can be a presence effect.

The crossed receipts on the same child include a wrong-image arm, and neither shows real beating wrong:

| probe | template | real | wrong | blank |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ID (a) | full | 0.393 | 0.4005 | 0.3625 |
| ID (b) | fast | 0.356 | 0.361 | 0.3035 |

These establish no useful image identity on these ID items.

**What stands now.** It is still open whether the model fails to acquire or use identity-conditioned answer signal at all, or acquires it and fails to transfer. The failure is not localized to transfer alone.

This does not change the successor plan. It strengthens the case for the common-prefix balanced complementary treatment already adopted, because that treatment targets identity-conditioned signal directly. The earlier entries stay as written. This entry supersedes their inference.

### 2026-09-28 08:41 AM LA — Next after `caption-truncation-census-20260928`
- **Successor hypothesis / not closed:** AMENDED per this seat mail 38464 (methodological correction, arrived after mail 38465's report but before this update): the original stem_survives/options_only classification was a binary substring-match heuristic that presupposed a conclusion. Corrected measurement uses exact tokenizer per-token character offsets (Tokenizer.encode(...).offsets) to classify each over-cap row's question-stem retention as complete/partial/none, counts options fully retained, and checks whether the answer-letter's own option text survives -- broken down per dataset_id. Receipt: seat record: caption-truncation-census-20260928.json (schema v2).

Frozen claim, reworded per this seat's instruction: truncation occurs at rate 68.97% (121736/176518 answer-scoped rows), with retention distribution: 0% of over-cap rows retain the COMPLETE question stem in any dataset; the majority retain it PARTIALLY (arxivqa 41.7% partial/58.3% none -- arxivqa is 77% of all rows and 97% of all over-cap rows; ai2d 77.5%/22.5%; scienceqa 75%/25%; m3exam-english 63.4%/36.6%; mmbench-dev-scienceqa 70.9%/29.1%). Options are mostly fully retained (82.6%-93.0% across datasets) and the answer-letter's own option text stays intact 82.6%-96.5% of the time. This is NOT "options-only" (the prior wording) and this census does not itself establish D-cause -- it establishes the truncation rate and retention shape only.

### 2026-09-28 08:23 AM LA — SUPPORTED: `caption-truncation-census-20260928`
- **Hypothesis:** encoded[-58:] keeps only the LAST 58 tokens of the answer-scoped caption. If the question stem is at the front and options/answer are appended after it, any caption whose total token length exceeds 58 has its stem silently dropped from the kept tail -- the model trains and is scored on options-only (or partial options) context, with the loss-bearing answer-letter target still computed as if the full MC prompt were present. This would explain any measured gap between eval-time full-context scoring and training-time truncated scoring, and any anomaly in the answer-letter round-trip if the kept tail's tokenization boundary lands mid-token.
- **Frozen criterion:** REFUTED if fraction of answer-scoped captions exceeding 58 tokens is 0 (or negligible, <1%) AND the kept-tail question-stem-survival fraction is ~100% -- i.e. truncation essentially never bites in the actual manifest distribution, so the mechanism the counterpart seat found is real code but inert in practice
- **Ruling:** refuted-if required <1% over-cap AND ~100% stem survival; observed 68.97% of answer-scoped captions (121736/176518) exceed the 58-token cap, and of those over-cap rows only 0.013% (16/121736) retain any surviving stem token -- 99.99% are options-only. Truncation is not an edge case, it is the majority behavior.
- **Receipt:** `seat record: caption-truncation-census-20260928.json`

### 2026-09-27 06:42 PM LA — MMMU anatomy: a missing image means answering B; any image spreads the answers; content barely matters
Understanding artifact on the attempt-14 child's r4 MMMU predictions (847 items, all three image conditions). Script: `seat record: mmmu_condition_anatomy.py`.

- **Missing image means one letter.** With no image the model answers `B` on 727 of 847 items (86%). Missing scores 0.283 only because `B` is 27% of the gold answers. It is a letter prior, not reasoning.
- **Any image spreads the answers, and its content barely matters.** Real: A 247, C 242, B 241, D 105, E 12. Wrong: C 264, A 239, B 214, D 115, E 15. Real and wrong pick the same letter on 81.4% of items; real and missing agree on 31.5%. The model responds to an image being PRESENT, far more than to what it shows.
- **Real is near chance given its own letter mix.** Matching the prediction marginals against the gold marginals (A 242, B 230, C 198, D 149, E 25) gives about 0.249 expected. Observed real is 0.263, wrong is 0.253, and the constant-A floor is 0.2857.
- **Per subject:** real beats missing most in Electronics (+0.19), Geography (+0.18), Biology, Management and Pharmacy (+0.11 to +0.14). Missing wins in Art Theory, Music, Mechanical Engineering, Diagnostics, Art and Accounting (+0.13 to +0.30). Those are the subjects where gold is concentrated on `B`. Subject counts are 16 to 30 items, so every per-subject difference sits inside about ±0.18 (1 SE).

**What this changes.** Gate D needs real > max(0.2857, missing + 0.0155, wrong + 0.0155), so real must reach about 0.30. That is roughly +0.05 of CONTENT-grounded accuracy above today's chance level. A mixture change that only makes the model more confident with an image present (a presence effect) will move real and wrong together and cannot cure D. The in-distribution result (0.5335 real vs 0.296 blank) shows the model can use image content on items like its training data, so the gap is transfer to college-level content. The next D run is judged first by real-minus-wrong, not real alone.

**Prediction registered for Q2(a) (college-level visual MC, bounded run):** real 0.275 [0.245, 0.310]; wrong 0.260 [0.235, 0.290]; real-minus-wrong 0.015 [-0.010, 0.045]; missing stays a letter prior (top letter share > 60%). Probability of clearing the D cure bar in one bounded run: 0.2.

### 2026-09-27 05:23 PM LA — Directives: predict and understand the network; autonomous execution; elastic host use
- **Why #1945 is not complete.** Gate D (MMMU) is a gap in understanding how Ember's image path responds to its
  data. It learns in-distribution (0.5335 real vs 0.296 blank) and does not transfer to college-level MMMU items.
  For seven attempts we changed the mixture without predicting MMMU first, and the image controls were inert.
  Gates A and B are a systems gap: the optimizer runs serialized after backward, plus a stream-event race under
  overlap. That is not a data gap.
- **Operating changes from today:**
  - Every training or eval run registers a numeric prediction with an interval, and the index keeps calibration.
  - Cheap dose-response probes run before any hour.
  - The execution seat runs continuously and dispatches cure legs itself.
  - Ember's memory and compute are to flex with host state instead of locking the device, with the
    measurement arithmetic recalibrated before any elastic number is quoted.
- **T1 correction (execution seat, 05:09 PM LA).** The crash is not an admission-set race: the resident set never
  moves per step. With deferred verdicts on, the optimizer runs on the default stream, but the completion event is
  recorded on the overlap stream. The next forward can therefore start while parameters are still being written.
  Cure: record and wait on the event of the stream the optimizer actually ran on. A red test comes first.

## 2026-09-28 12:40 PM LA — Interpretation limits for the gate D corrected pair (pre-registered, not a criterion)

Frozen criteria are unchanged. These rules say what each outcome licenses, and were written before the learner runs.

1. Control passes protected accuracy and image dependence, candidate fails: a candidate-specific limitation (reduced-template learning).
2. Both fail: shared input, objective, optimization or representation explanations stay OPEN, not established. The shared parent (dispatch 103619Z, keep 0,6,12,18,23) was trained under the reduced template, so the full-template control can start with a predictive disadvantage in re-enabled layers. Matched storage is not matched starting quality.
3. C passes but image dependence fails: NLL alone is not evidence of useful visual conditioning.
4. C and D pass but A or B fails: keep that exact learning result; later changes are restricted to proven-equivalent execution changes.
Endpoint C deltas are not learning gains from a matched predictive baseline.

## 2026-10-04 01:38 PM LA — Correction: the H19 hour was not "fresh-only", and what "head stays 161e9b2b" means

Earlier entries call the H19 lineage hour "fresh-only" or "never-seen" text (the hypothesis lines for the H20 interleave, among others). the counterpart seat's receipt 12326de1 shows the H19 hour also consumed older-source positions alongside the fresh Wikipedia files. Those words are withdrawn as descriptions of what H19 trained on. The frozen hypotheses and criteria that used them stay byte-identical, because they were frozen before this was known. Their rulings stand on their own receipts.

The older-source positions in H19 are not automatically verified replay. Whether any of them repeat earlier-hour exposure is not established by these receipts, and no retention or forgetting reading may assume it.

"Head stays 161e9b2b" names the digest of the head pointer file. The `current_head_sha256` value written inside that file, reported as eb3b4366, is the digest of the head checkpoint manifest it names. These are two different quantities: one identifies the pointer record, and the other identifies the model it points at. A head line now quotes both.

This is an evidence correction only. It is separate from the files 6 to 10 admission review and grants nothing there.
